### 📏 STANDING RULE — FE layout IS verifiable here (learned 2026-08-01, TASK-081)

**We were wrong that the in-app browser can't check layout.** The pane doesn't *paint*, but it still **computes
layout**: `getBoundingClientRect()` returns real widths and `resize_window` genuinely reflows the page. Fern
reproduced the REQ-024 date-input defect **by measurement** — the two inputs were **26 px and 36 px** where the
fix gives **176 px**, which is the stakeholder's screenshot arrived at from a number.

**So:** any FE change that **adds or resizes a control in a shared row** must **measure the row at 1600 / 1280 /
768 / 375 and report the numbers**. That converts "it looks fine" into evidence.

**Still out of reach:** colour, contrast, overlap, font rendering — anything *painted*. **A deployed look
remains the only full detector**, which is an argument for deploying in small slices rather than for reviewing
harder. (Sober had told the team layout was structurally unverifiable and to compensate with prose; that was
wrong, and this rule replaces it.)

| LINE webhook | ✅ now points here (moved 2026-07-30) | was pointed here until 2026-07-30 |

> ## 🚦 STANDING RULE — the two environments and how work flows between them (owner, 2026-08-16)
>
> The owner named them, so these are the names the team uses from now on. **Stop writing "prod"** — he does not use
> the word, and the mismatch already cost us a day of confusion:
>
> | | **`sid`** — where we build | **`uat`** — the customer's system |
> |---|---|---|
> | frontoffice | **som.develyst.online** | **frontoffice.develyst.online** |
> | backoffice | **backoffice-som.develyst.online** | **backoffice.develyst.online** |
> | who touches it | the team's work is verified here | **owner only** — the team never runs anything against it |
>
> **The flow is one-directional and there is no third stop: build → verify on `sid` → deploy to `uat`.**
>
> 🔴 **MIGRATION DISCIPLINE — the owner's words: *"หากเรามีการ migrate ก็ต้องลองที่ sid ก่อน ห้ามพลาด เพราะหลังเทสเสร็จ
> ต้องขึ้น uat เลย"***. Every migration is **run and verified on `sid` first**, and only then on `uat`. There is no
> rehearsal after this point: **from the moment REQ-055 lands, `uat` holds the customer's real families and their
> real money records** — a failed migration there is not a rollback exercise, it is their business. The existing
> `db:verify` / witness-ledger discipline (REQ-032) is the mechanism; this rule is when it must be exercised.
> **Whoever writes a migration TASK states, in the TASK, how it was proven on `sid`.**
>
> 🟠 **"sid first" is for SCHEMA/CODE migrations — NOT for data imports (Sober, corrected by Porter 2026-08-22).**
> A **migration** changes structure/behaviour identically on both boxes, so rehearsing on `sid` protects `uat`. A
> **data import** writes *different rows per box*, and the two boxes hold *different data*: `sid` already carries the
> full old-file rehearsal (130 students, old names), `uat` carries only what has been committed there. Re-running an
> import batch on `sid` from a **newer** file walks into the REQ-059 rename problem and **duplicates** — the very
> thing the import is trying to avoid. ⇒ **For a data-import TASK, the run target is decided by which box lacks the
> rows, not by the sid-first reflex; state the target explicitly in the TASK/runsheet.** REQ-055 wave-1's four
> batches were correctly `uat`-only for exactly this reason.

**✅ This split is INTENTIONAL — confirmed by the stakeholder 2026-07-30. It is not a problem to fix.**
We are **pre-first-launch**. The customer uses **`frontoffice.develyst.online` (production, the OLD version)** to
**try the product and send comments**; the team builds and verifies everything on **`sid`**. Production will be
brought up to date at the **first real serve** — no rush, and the stakeholder has explicitly parked it.

**What the team must still keep in mind:**
- **"DELIVERED" currently means "verified on `sid`"** — not yet live for the customer. Everything since
  ~2026-07-16 (REQ-001/002/004/005/006/007/008/010/011/015/016) exists on `sid` only.
- 🔑 **Customer feedback arrives from the OLD version.** Before turning a customer comment into a REQ, **check

- The two servers have **separate databases**, so data diverges as well as code.
- The eventual **first-serve deploy is a real event, not a routine push**: production is behind on both code and
  **DB migrations**, and has its own DB — it will need a planned sequence (incl. the REQ-006 `bo`/shared-DB
  change). Flagged early; not scheduled.

## 🚀 BATCH 2026-08-02 — READY. Migration check RE-RUN today: scheduling **18 = 18**, backoffice **6 = 6** ✅

🔴 **THIS BATCH CARRIES THREE MIGRATIONS** (scheduling only; backoffice has none new). All additive
(`IF NOT EXISTS`, defaults) so they are safe to apply **before** the new code — old code simply ignores them.

| Migration | Adds | For |
|---|---|---|
| `0015_teacher_link_requests` | new table `teacher_link_requests` | REQ-020 Stage 2 — teacher link approval |
| `0016_subjects_price_group` | `subjects.price_group` + backfill by subject **name** | per-program pricing (REQ-027/029) |
| `0017_entitlement_source` | `course_packages.source` · `vouchers.source` = `SALE`\|`IMPORT` | REQ-025 — import ≠ sale |

⚠️ **`0016` backfills by matching subject NAME exactly.** Any subject on `sid` whose name differs even slightly
keeps `price_group = NULL`. **Check for NULLs after migrating** — a null price group is how a program silently
loses its prices later.

### The order (do not improvise it)

1. **`bun run db:migrate`** in `smart-scheduler-back` — **before** restarting anything.
1b. 🔴 **`bun run db:verify` — BLOCKING. Do not restart the app until it prints ✅.**
   **`db:migrate` can print *"migrations applied successfully"* and exit 0 having applied NOTHING** (the ledger
   holds a row newer than the migration — TASK-085/086). **This is not theoretical: it took the customer's
   calendar down on BOTH boxes on 2026-08-24** — `0022_booking_attendee_note` was skipped silently, the app was
   restarted against a schema without the column, and every `/api/calendar` read returned 500.
   **If `db:verify` is red:** `bun run db:seed-ledger` (dry-run) → read it → `--apply` → **`db:migrate` again**
   → `db:verify` ✅. Seeding first is what lets `migrate` see the skipped migration at all.
2. **Deploy `smart-scheduler-back` (:4006)** → `pm2 restart`.
3. **Deploy `smart-scheduler-front` (:3016)** — **in the same sitting, not tomorrow.**
4. `backoffice-back` (:4010) + `backoffice-front` (:3018) for TASK-083/084 — **no migration**, order free.

### 🔴 Why steps 2 and 3 must not be separated — three pairs, all "a gate without its screen"

| Pair | Backend alone would… |
|---|---|
| **TASK-075 + 076** | teacher self-linking now **queues for approval** — without the approval screen **nobody can link at all**, with 22 of ~23 teachers due to link this fortnight |
| **TASK-077 + 078** | only sellable (program, size) combinations exist — an old FE still offering 4/6/10 for every program would send combinations the server now rejects |
| **TASK-079 + 080** | harmless alone (new verbs, unused) — but it ships with the other two |

**One rule covers all three, and it is the one this project keeps re-learning:**
> 🟠 **Never ship a new server-side gate without the screen that opens it.**

  teacher can then use the bot. This replaces the hole where **claiming a nickname granted access unverified.**
- **Course creation** — Onewheel offers **no 10 h**, Balance Play offers **no 4 h**.
- **Prices** — 🔴 **`sale:ensure-items` has NOT been run and must not be until the real prices are in it.**
  Confirm the items carry the **real, VAT-inclusive** card prices, not `฿1,390 × hours`.
- **REQ-024's custom date range** (TASK-081) — the From/To inputs must be usable, not collapsed.
- **The old Dashboard nav entry is gone** (TASK-082); SOM dashboard / Daily report / Needs attention unaffected.

### 🔴 MIGRATION CHECK — read this before every single deploy. No exceptions.

**2026-08-01: the production site went down (`GET /teachers` → 500, every `bo.item` query dead) because this
manifest said "✅ no DB migration in this batch".** That was true when written. **TASK-066 then added
`0005_bo_item_external_ref` and nobody updated this line.** The stakeholder deployed trusting it, the new code
queried a column the database didn't have, and the frontoffice was unusable until the migration was run.

**The rule, and why it is phrased this way:**

> 🔴 **"No migration" is a CLAIM, not a state. It expires the moment another task lands.**
> **Nobody may write or repeat it without re-counting the migration files at that moment.**

A "nothing to do here" line is the single most dangerous cell in this table, because it is the one people act on
**without checking**. Every other row invites verification; this one invites trust.

**The 10-second check — run it before any deploy** (needs no database access):

```bash
ls H:/scheduler/smart-scheduler-back/drizzle/*.sql | wc -l          # vs "tag" count in meta/_journal.json
ls H:/scheduler/smart-scheduler-backoffice-back/drizzle/*.sql | wc -l
```

- **File count must equal the `"tag"` count in that repo's `drizzle/meta/_journal.json`.** A `.sql` file missing
  from the journal is **silently skipped** by `db:migrate` (TASK-042).
- Then compare the newest file against what is already applied on the server. **If a newer migration exists,
  this batch HAS a migration** — say so in the table, and run it **before** restarting the app.
- ✅ **Audited 2026-08-01 after the outage: scheduling 15 = 15, backoffice 6 = 6. No unregistered migrations.**
- ✅ **Re-audited 2026-08-03 (Sober, deploy-gate) on branch `dong`: scheduling `.sql` 18 = 18 journal tags, backoffice 6 = 6. No unregistered migrations. Split ledgers confirmed in both `drizzle.config.ts` (`__drizzle_migrations_scheduling` / `__drizzle_migrations_bo`); `db:migrate` = `drizzle-kit migrate && db:verify`, and `verify-migrations.ts` exits 1 on a silent skip.**

**Two commands, and they are NOT interchangeable — Porter got this wrong live, during an outage:**
| Command | Repo | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **`bun run db:migrate`** | the repo owning the schema | **Applies the `.sql` schema migrations.** ← this is the one that creates columns |
| `bun run migrate:bo` | `backoffice-back` | **Moves DATA** `ops.*` → `bo.*`. Creates nothing. Will itself fail if the schema is behind. |

⚠️ Both backends share one `__drizzle_migrations` table, so a migration **can be skipped without erroring.**
**Never accept "the command said success" as proof — load the actual page and confirm.**

| REQ-032 | 🔴 Migrations must never fail silently — and the two repos must stop sharing one ledger | 🔴 **HIGHEST** | ✅ **DELIVERED 2026-08-02** — TASK-085/086/087; both ledgers split + witness-based seeding + self-verifying `db:migrate`; scheduling recovered, backoffice reconciled, the two retired `ops` entries named every run | **@Sober — top of the queue, ahead of everything.** Owner 2026-08-02, after the second `sid` outage in two days: *"ทำความเข้าใจว่าปัญหาเกิดจากอะไร แล้วแก้ให้ไม่เกิดขึ้นอีก แบบนี้ไปเกิดที่ production ไม่ได้เด็ดขาด"*. **Root cause measured, three layers:** (1) neither `drizzle.config.ts` sets `migrationsTable`/`migrationsSchema`, so **both backends share `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations`** in one DB; (2) the migrator applies only entries **newer than the last recorded `created_at`** — it assumes one linear history, which two repos make false; (3) `when` values are **hand-authored** (because `db:generate` is unusable — the TASK-042 finding) and the two series were never coordinated: scheduling's max is **1783000000013**, backoffice's is **1785542400000**. ⇒ **backoffice `0003`/`0004` (1784531451038/39) were ALREADY above scheduling's entire journal — the wall went up when backoffice FIRST migrated (the REQ-006 rebuild), NOT yesterday (Porter's timeline was wrong; Jason corrected it). So scheduling migrations have been silently un-appliable for far longer than a day, and NO environment's schema can be assumed to match its journal**, silently, exit 0. **Connected only through a table neither repo knows it shares.** 🔴 **`smart-scheduler-back` cannot apply ANY migration now, on any environment** — and **production has never had 0015–0017 either**, so this is a production defect that `sid` found for us, not a `sid` problem. ⚠️ **Delicate:** a fresh ledger would replay from `0000`; the shared one no longer describes either repo. **Requirement, not design:** a skipped migration must **fail loudly**; each repo owns its history; already-applied migrations must not re-run; **the API should not start healthy against a schema that is behind** (both outages presented as 500s on unrelated screens — a *finance* column killed the *teacher* list); and the owner must be able to answer *"did it land?"* in one step without reading Postgres errors. ❓ Refuse-to-start vs warn (Porter's lean: refuse — but a wrong strict check takes the app down by itself, so it's your call) · **production's real migration state is unknown and must be established, not guessed** · has anyone checked for a **third** consumer of that shared table? |

COMPLETE 2026-08-01: TASK-064 ✅ · 065 ✅ · 066 ✅ · 067 ✅ · 068 ✅ · 083 ✅ · 084 ✅.** ⚠️ **Acceptance is one check:** open a real month and confirm the screen's own reconciliation — **buckets + unattributed = month total**, green badge — and that the reason amounts sum to the unattributed total. Both were only ever exercised against a stub. ⚠️ **And it can only report what was posted** — ฿0 until TASK-066 is deployed and `sale:ensure-items` has run, then forward-only. ✅ **REQ-014's remaining blocker is being closed by TASK-077** — per-program pricing makes the sale record the program, so revenue-by-sport becomes a **direct read of `external_ref`** instead of a join back through refId → course → bookings → subject. Better than SPEC-021's original design: a course sale's sport becomes known **at the moment of sale** rather than inferred later. I will rewrite SPEC-021's attribution to prefer the product code once TASK-077 lands, keeping the refId join only as a fallback for pre-rework sales. 🔐 **+ @Jason TASK-068: the P&L is readable with NO token** — every write in `bo.ts` is guarded, all reads are open, so this REQ's own access-control answer ("finance is executive-only because it lives on the backoffice") is **false at the API level**. Found by Jason while building TASK-064; the UI is not protection. _History:_ 🔴 **Jason blocked TASK-064 on a premise check and was right: no sale has been recorded since the REQ-006 rebuild (2026-07-28)** — `catalogRoutes` is mounted nowhere so `recordSale` 404s, and both call sites are `void recordSale(...)`, i.e. best-effort **by design**, so it failed with no voice. Built as specced the report would return **฿0 with the sum check PASSING** (0+0===0) — a green, empty, technically-correct revenue screen. **Two of the three breaks are MY errors:** (1) `bo.item.externalRef` never existed — I asserted a column from a function signature instead of opening the schema; (2) **"retroactive coverage over every historical sale" is NOT achievable** and I sold it as a reason to prefer the design — repair attributes sales **going forward only** — **@Porter correct this before คุณปุ้ม hears it.** → **TASK-066** (repair the write path, direct Drizzle like the freelance ceiling, + `external_ref` migration + the missing course/voucher INCOME items + **loud** failure logging) → **TASK-067** (8th digest check so it can never be silent again) → then TASK-064/065. SPEC-021 (2026-08-01). Design = **derive by joining, do NOT tag sales going forward and do NOT explode the item catalogue into per-subject income items**: every sale is a `bo.movement` `refType:"SALE"` whose **item product code** says what `refId` points at (`course-{size}`→course, `first-trial`/`single-session`→booking, `voucher-{hours}`→voucher), so **one attribution map** `(code, refId) → {studentId, subjectId or null}` answers **both** metrics — grouped by subject for revenue, by student for spend. **No migration, retroactive over every historical sale, nothing to keep in sync.** backoffice-back adds **read-only** `public` declarations (same DB since REQ-006; mirrors the direction REQ-006 already chose) — **it never writes or migrates `public`**. 🔴 **A voucher is structurally unattributable** (generic hours; its sessions may be different sports) ⇒ the report carries an explicit **`unattributed`** bucket and **`buckets + unattributed === total` is asserted by a test** — a finance report that silently drops what it cannot classify shows a tidy split that does not add up to the real month, and that is the number an executive acts on. ⚠️ **This delivers revenue by SPORT only — "ยอดตามสาขา / onsite-vs-online" remains structurally impossible** (REQ-021 finding 9: badges are on bookings, `bo.movement` has no badge link, no per-booking price) — @Porter please make sure that is not what คุณปุ้ม expects. **Method question answered without routing**; **one real finance question raised (non-blocking): when should voucher revenue belong to a sport?** — recognising it per session as consumed changes *when* revenue is recognised, which is คุณปุ้ม's call, not mine. _Porter's original:_ Sober — spec. **⚠️ RBAC DISSOLVED (stakeholder 2026-07-29): NO new role system** — the backoffice login **is** the executive's; only executives access the backoffice; front staff stay on the frontoffice → finance-on-backoffice = already executive-only (REQ-002). So REQ-014 = just **revenue-by-sport + per-customer spend on the backoffice**. Monthly revenue total HAVE; **split-by-sport PARTIAL** (sale carries no subject → SA tags sport on sales); per-customer spend PARTIAL (finance join). **No open business Q — UNBLOCKED.** |

| REQ-017 | Teacher bookings → phone calendar (subscription feed) [LINE-C] | MEDIUM | ⏸️ **DEPLOYED — acceptance INCOMPLETE, parked by stakeholder 2026-07-31** | **NOT delivered.** Deployed to `sid` (migration 0013 + `PUBLIC_CALENDAR_BASE_URL` + restart) and the teacher **does receive a private subscription link** from the LINE "ปฏิทิน" button ✅. **Blocked on real-device UX, not on the feed:** (1) **LINE does not linkify `webcal://`** — it links only from `develyst.online…`, dropping the scheme *and* the `som.` subdomain, so tapping fails; (2) the **Google Calendar mobile app cannot add a calendar by URL at all** (desktop web only), so Android/Google teachers realistically can't subscribe. `calendarUrls()` already returns **both** `https` and `webcal` — only `webcal` is sent today. **Whether events actually render was never confirmed.** Stakeholder: "ช่างมันไปก่อน" — revisit later. _Previously:_ **@Porter — deploy + acceptance.** TASK-044 **DONE** (Sober-verified 2026-07-31: journal audit 14=14, auth-boundary, stable `UID`, `STATUS:CANCELLED`, UTF-8-octet folding; tsc 0 / suite 173/0). **Deploy needs THREE steps:** (1) `bun run db:migrate` (0013) · (2) **set `PUBLIC_CALENDAR_BASE_URL`** — without it the link is relative and useless on a phone · (3) restart :4006. Then a teacher gets the link via LINE (`ปฏิทิน` / quick reply) or staff via `POST /api/teachers/:id/calendar-link` (`?rotate=true` kills the old link). ⚠️ Set expectations: **Google refreshes subscribed feeds slowly (hours)**; iPhone ~15 min. _Design:_ SPEC-014 (2026-07-30) — **per-teacher tokenized `.ics`** — `GET /api/calendar/:token.ics` mounted **auth-free** (same pattern as `publicCheckin`, before `authMiddleware`), window −30d/+90d, **stable `UID` per booking** so edits update rather than duplicate, `STATUS:CANCELLED` included so cancellations disappear from the phone, Asia/Bangkok with explicit TZ. Token = `teachers.calendar_token` (unique, rotatable; old link 404s) via migration **0013, journal-registered per the TASK-042 rule — no `db:generate`**. Link delivered via LINE (`ปฏิทิน`/`calendar` + quick reply) or a staff endpoint. **FYI → @Porter (non-blocking):** Google refreshes subscribed feeds **slowly (hours)** vs iPhone (~15 min) — fine per the AC, but worth telling คุณฟีน; near-real-time on Android = a separate heavier Google-API REQ. |

> independent, can run in parallel) → **REQ-012** (registration/demographics form opened from LINE, needs REQ-015)
> → **REQ-013** (SOM dashboard, needs REQ-012 for demographics) → **REQ-014** (backoffice revenue/spend, independent).
> Still open earlier in the queue: **REQ-009** (freelance type-change close). REQ-010/011 DELIVERED.

> ⚠️ **STRATEGIC PIVOT (2026-07-20, คุณฟีน):** current **backoffice is being torn down &
> rebuilt from scratch** (hard to use / not scalable). Finish the **frontoffice first,
> stand-alone**. Freelance limit → moves into frontoffice (REQ-004). Freelance **P&L/expense
> + FT/PT salary = deferred** to the new backoffice. REQ-003 (teacher↔ops sync) ON HOLD.
> REQ-001/002 stay DELIVERED but their backoffice-side money features are superseded by the rebuild.

> SPECs written 2026-07-20: **SPEC-001** (freelance budget-stock, booking-time cap
> & expense) + **SPEC-002** (FT/PT recurring effective-dated fixed-cost salary),
> both from REQ-001. 6 buildable tasks cut; TASK-007 (day-end revenue) blocked on
> a revenue-recognition question to Porter (does NOT block the other 6).

> Decision (2026-07-20, คุณฟีน): backoffice = **Path A, item-centric P&L**. No full
> payroll engine, no student hour-wallet. Freelance pay = per-teacher monthly
> "budget-stock" drawn down at the end-of-day cut → auto-disable at cap. Full/part-time
> = manual `FIXED_COST`. See REQ-001 + `project-understanding.md` §6.

Fix = reconcile **whole-booking**: release any item holding it that isn't the current teacher's, then apply the normal target | — | ✅ **DONE** (Sober 2026-08-03 — code-verified on branch `dong` once the office tree synced: **tsc 0 · bun test 396/0, run by me**. Per-item idempotency key confirmed in code (`applyHoldMove:160` `fl:<booking>:<item>:held<0\|1>`); `reconcileBookingHolds` is whole-booking, target from `heldTarget` only, release never cap-blocked; **the regression surface I flagged is clean** — `updateBookingStatus:1255` uses the same reconcile and collapses to one adjustment when no teacher moved; `moveBooking:1315` is transactional. ⏳ **stale-hold DATA REQUEST routed to @Porter** — read-only count of bookings held on >1 `bo.item`; repair is `PATCH`-with-current-`teacherId`, no bespoke SQL.) — built by Jason 2026-08-02: ✅ **`moveBooking` is the ONLY writer of an existing booking's `teacherId`** — grepped every `update/insert(bookings)`; the others are the check-in token, `NO_SHOW`, and four status writes, none touching `teacherId`. Near-miss named: the sick-leave **extension** inserts a *new* booking carrying `current.teacherId`, but it's a different id created `PENDING` (holds nothing) ⇒ no risk. 🔴 **I found a SECOND bug while building this: the idempotency key wasn't per-item.** It was `fl:<booking>:held<target>` — unique per (booking,target) but **not per item** — so on a round trip **A→B→A** the release of B and the earlier release of A both key `…:held0`, `onConflictDoNothing` swallows the second, and the booking ends up **held on TWO items at once**. Exactly the round-trip off-by-one you warned about, and it would have survived a correct `planHoldMoves`. Key now includes the item; safe to change because `held` is derived from the **movement ledger**, never from the key. **Built `planHoldMoves(holds, currentItemId, target)`** — pure: *release every item holding this booking that isn't the current teacher's, then bring the current teacher's to target* — correct for **any** number of moves, and it collapses to today's behaviour when the teacher hasn't moved. ⚠️ **It decides WHICH item, never HOW MANY hours** — `heldTarget`/`reconcileDelta` still own that, asserted by a test comparing its deltas to `reconcileDelta`'s. **I REPLACED `reconcileFreelanceDraw` rather than adding alongside it** — you said make the reconcile whole-booking, and keeping both would be two definitions of one reconcile with one subtly wrong; `updateBookingStatus` now uses it too and produces an identical single adjustment when nothing moved. **`moveBooking` is now transactional — it wasn't** (a bare `db.update`), so the teacher write and the money move can no longer disagree. ⚠️ One deliberate asymmetry: **a release is never blocked by a cap** — refusing it because a ceiling is full would strand the old teacher's budget forever. ⚠️ **Your second check — stale data: I did NOT query** (and after TASK-085 I won't). Signature is exact: **more than one `bo.item` with a non-zero net hold for the same `ref_id`**, arising when a booking was moved between two FREELANCE teachers while `CONFIRMED`/`ATTENDED`/`SICK_LEAVE`/`EXTENDED`. **Read-only query written in the notes for @Porter to route.** **No repair written**, per your instruction — but worth knowing the fix needs **no bespoke SQL**: `PATCH` each affected booking with its current `teacherId` and the new reconcile self-corrects (there's a test collapsing a two-item state to one). **tsc 0 · `bun test` 396/0** (was 384, **+12**) — incl. **A→B→A→B ending with exactly one hour on B and every intermediate step holding exactly one, never zero, never two**; tests assert on the **applied result**, not just the emitted moves, because a plan can look right and still leave the wrong state. | Jason | — |

| ⚠️ **`env -u DATABASE_URL bun run …` does NOT isolate a repo from production** | **Everyone — @Porter please raise it to PROTOCOL level** (found by Jason 2026-08-02, self-reported) | **Bun auto-loads `.env`**, so unsetting the variable in the shell does **not** stop a script connecting — and this repo's `.env` points at the real `sid` host. Jason hit this trying to prove two new scripts merely *parse*; he expected the `DATABASE_URL required` guard to trip. It didn't. He audited from source and reported it **before** the work: both runs were **read-only** (two SELECTs; the split script's writes all sit after its `if (!apply)` exit — I verified that in code), so no harm — **but "it turned out read-only" is only knowable *after* the connection, and the next script might not be.** ⚠️ **This beats a deliberate safety measure, so it will catch anyone** — it affects Fern's dev-server work and QA too, not just BE. **Nothing from those runs counts as a sanctioned DATA REQUEST answer.** |
| 🔴 **OUTAGE 2026-08-02 — scheduling migrations are silently skipped (shared ledger)** | ✅ **SCHEDULING RECOVERED 2026-08-02** — ledger seeded from the schema, `0015–0017` applied, verify green. The witness approach worked and **the dry-run stopped the operator twice before anything was changed**. 🔴 **Backoffice paused on a scope call, now decided → @Jason TASK-087:** its `0001`/`0002` touch **only the retired `ops` schema**, so they get a **third verdict — `retired`**: not applied, **not silently excluded**, printed every run, verify green. _History:_ **TASK-086.** TASK-085 ✅ built the split + guard, and **the dry-run did its job: the owner STOPPED at step 1.** It found **9** unrecorded journal entries, not 3 — and **6 of them (`0006·0009·0010·0011·0013·0014`) are demonstrably live** (parents, `job_runs`, archiving, freelance budgets, calendar tokens, the demographics being entered all week). **The script is faithful to the ledger; the ledger is not faithful to the database.** `0004`/`0005` also appear **twice** (legacy-tag + hash) ⇒ **a ledger written by two mechanisms cannot be assumed complete.** → TASK-086 reconciles from the **schema** instead. ⚠️ **Two corrections to the re-run analysis (Sober read the files):** `0011`'s FK **IS** wrapped in `EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object` so it is re-run safe; **`0006` is confirmed un-re-runnable** — not the drops (`IF EXISTS`) but the **backfill reading `students.phone`**, a column the same file drops. **Exactly one migration cannot survive a re-run, not two.** | **Both repos share one `__drizzle_migrations`** (neither config sets `migrationsTable`), and the migrator applies only journal entries newer than the ledger's single newest row — **it does not compare hashes**. backoffice `0005` (`when` **1785542400000**) is newer than scheduling's entire journal (newest **1783000000013**), so `db:migrate` in scheduling applies **nothing and exits 0**. ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-02 (Jason, accepted):** the block **predates backoffice `0005`** — `0003`/`0004` (1784531451038/39) were already above scheduling's whole journal, so the wall went up when backoffice's `db:migrate` **first ran** during the REQ-006 rebuild. *"Yesterday's fix caused today's outage" is wrong and I repeated it* — which also means **scheduling's migrations have been silently un-appliable for longer than a day**, so don't assume the DB matches the journal anywhere else. ⚠️ **Not a one-off: scheduling can never apply another migration** until it leapfrogs, and the next backoffice migration re-breaks it — **so bumping timestamps is a race, not a fix.** Immediate action is the **binary rollback** Porter gave the owner (correct: 0015–0017 never applied, so the DB matches the previous build exactly — and **no SQL**). Fix = per-repo `migrationsTable` + **split the existing ledger by hash** (the truth is already in it; copying beats reconstructing) + a post-migrate check that **exits non-zero on an unrecorded journal entry**. |

| ⛔ **TASK-064 BLOCKED — SPEC-021's input doesn't exist** (raised 2026-08-01 by Jason) | **Sober** (2 routing/schema calls + 1 DATA REQUEST to route up) | **There are no `bo.movement` rows with `refType:"SALE"` to attribute, and `bo.item` has no `externalRef` to key on.** Built as specced the report returns `totalMinor: 0` with an empty split — and **`buckets + unattributed === total` PASSES (0+0===0)**, so the DoD's own correctness check is satisfied by an empty report an executive would read as "฿0 revenue". Three traced breaks: **(1)** `recordSale` POSTs to `/api/v1/catalog/items/by-ref/movements`, whose `catalogRoutes` is **never imported** — `api.ts` mounts `/auth` + `/bo` only and says the `ops.*` routes are retired ⇒ 404, and both call sites are `void recordSale(...)`, **best-effort so it fails silently**. Nothing has written a SALE row since the REQ-006 rebuild (TASK-027). **(2)** even alive, that route wrote **`ops.stock_movements`**, not `bo.movement`; `migrate-to-bo.ts` was a **one-shot backfill** (`refType` at :111, ops `externalRef` → bo **`ownerRef`** at :78), not a standing bridge. **(3)** `bo.item` has `ownerRef`/`externalSource` but **no `externalRef`**, and the only INCOME items ever seeded are `first-trial`/`single-session` **into `ops.catalog_items`** — **`course-{size}` / `voucher-{hours}` items exist nowhere**, so the two highest-revenue codes would have no-op'd on item-not-found even before the route was unmounted. ✅ **Checked the escape hatch: revenue is NOT derivable from `public`** — `course_packages` stores only `size`, `vouchers` only `totalHours`, **neither has a price**; money lives solely in `bo.item.unitPriceMinor`. So the dependency is real and **SPEC-021's read-side design is not the problem — only its input is.** **Rec:** (A) repair the sale write path first under its own REQ — direct Drizzle on the shared DB like the freelance ceiling already does, which also kills the last retired-`ops` dependency; it touches **scheduling-back's write path** + needs priced `course-`/`voucher-` items in `bo.item`, so it **does not fit SPEC-021's read-only envelope** — then (B) TASK-064 unchanged. ⚠️ **Reset one expectation now: SPEC-021's "retroactive over every historical sale" is NOT achievable** — a repair attributes sales *going forward*; the past is limited to the one-shot backfill, which per (3) can't include courses or vouchers. Worth telling Porter **before** คุณปุ้ม sees a suspiciously small number. **No code written** — the map's key shape depends on the answer. Full evidence + Q1/Q2/Q3 in the task file. |
